hose covered under Requirement (8) above are those who are not covered under (1) through (7) above and whom
this graduate school has recognized, through individual screening of entrance qualifications, as having academic
abilities at least equivalent to a holder of a Master’s or Professional degree.
2) Those who wish to apply based on Requirement (8) above must submit the documents designated by the Graduate
School of Information Science and Technology to the Admissions Office (ref. Section 11 below) by Wednesday, May
13, 2026, in the case of Summer Entrance Examinations, and by Monday, October 26, 2026, in the case of Winter
Entrance Examinations, as the eligibility for application is confirmed individually in advance by document screening.
Applicants should inquire in advance at the Graduate School of Information Science and Technology Admissions
Office regarding the documents to be submitted.
3) Those who are recognized, through screening of entrance qualification, as having academic abilities at least
equivalent to a holder of a Master's or Professional degree will be permitted to apply for and take the entrance
examination.
3. Screening Process
Screening of the successful applicants for admission will be conducted by comprehensively taking into account
the results of the written examination, oral examination, submitted documents, and the applicant's Master's
thesis or its alternative.
(Note 1) The details of the screening process may differ for each department. Please refer to the Admission Guide for the
applicable department for detailed information.
(Note 2) As professional applicants may be eligible to apply for a Doctoral Program [Special Selection for Professionals],
they should also obtain the Admission Guide of Doctoral Program [Special Selection for Professionals] and refer
to the Admission Guide for the applicable department for detailed information.
3
The document is a translation from the official Japanese version.
In the case of conflict, the Japanese version shall prevail and be conclusive.
4. Examination Schedules and Locations
(1) Summer Entrance Examinations will be held during the period from Monday, August 24 through Tuesday,
September 1, 2026. For details of the examinations, refer to the Admission Guide for each department. The
examination schedule for those who are expected to complete the Master’s program of this graduate school
may differ from the above schedule depending on the department; follow the Admission Guide of each
department.
(2) Winter Entrance Examinations will be held between late January and mid-February 2027. For details of the
examinations, refer to the Admission Guide of each department. The examination schedule for those who
are expected to complete the Master’s program of this graduate school may differ from the above schedule
depending on the department; follow the Admission Guide of each department.

6. Application Procedures
(1) Applicants should complete the application form via the WEB Application System. All the required
documents must be uploaded as PDF files to the WEB Application System. Follow the instructions on the
admission webpage of the Graduate School of Information Science and Technology (admission webpage
https://www.i.u-tokyo.ac.jp/edu/entra/entra_e.shtml) and on the WEB application system. Access the
system from the admission webpage.
(2) Application Period
1) Summer Entrance Examinations
Applications are accepted from Friday, May 29, until 14:00 on Thursday, June 4, 2026 (JST).
2) Winter Entrance Examinations
Applications are accepted from Wednesday, November 11, until 14:00 on Tuesday, November 17, 2026
(JST).

8. Admission Notification and Enrollment Procedures
(1) Summer Entrance Examinations
1. The list of the successful applicants will be posted on the website of the Graduate School of Information
Science and Technology (https://www.i.u-tokyo.ac.jp/index_e.shtml the same hereafter) at approximately
15:00 on Wednesday, September 9, 2026.
2. The Letter of Acceptance for Admission for entry in October 2026 will be delivered via E-mail around early
September 2026 (TBD) after the screening for “The University of Tokyo Security Export Control” is
completed. The Letter of Acceptance for Admission for entry in April 2027 will be delivered via E-mail by
late October 2026 (TBD), after the screening for “The University of Tokyo Security Export Control” is
completed.
(2) Winter Entrance Examinations
1. The list of successful applicants will be posted on the website of the Graduate School of Information Science
and Technology at approximately 15:00 on Monday, February 15, 2027.
2. The Letter of Acceptance for Admission for entry in April 2027 will be delivered via E-mail around late
February 2027 (TBD) after the screening for “The University of Tokyo Security Export Control” is
completed. The Letter of Acceptance for Admission for entry in October 2027 will be delivered via E-mail
by late March 2027 (TBD), after the screening for “The University of Tokyo Security Export Control” is
completed.
(3) Applicants who have received the Letter of Acceptance for Admission should follow the required enrollment
procedures (i.e., payment of admission fee and submission of enrollment documents) by following the
instructions delivered separately. For entry in October 2026, these procedures must be completed by
Thursday, September 24, 2026 (TBD); for entry in April 2027, these procedures must be completed by
Thursday, March 11, 2027 (TBD); and for entry in October 2027, these procedures must be completed by
Wednesday, September 22, 2027 (TBD). Failure to complete the enrollment procedures by the designated
date will be considered cancellation of admission. The instructions for enrollment procedures will be
delivered via E-mail by early September 2026 for entry in October 2026, by late February 2027 for entry in
April 2027, and by early September for entry in October 2027.
(4) Admission Fee and Tuition (estimated amounts for AY2027)
1. Admission fee: 282,000 yen
2. Tuition for half-year: 260,400 yen (Annual amount: 520,800 yen)
(Note1) The fees indicated above are estimates. If the amounts are amended by the time of admission or during
enrollment, the new amounts will apply from the time of the amendment.
(Note2) These fees are waived for international students on the Japanese government (MEXT) scholarships.
(Note3) The admission fee will not be charged to those who complete a Master’s program or professional degree program
at our university immediately before the desired enrollment date (in September for those enrolling in October,
9

9. Financial Support for Students
Please refer to the following webpage for details.

The document is a translation from the official Japanese version.
In the case of conflict, the Japanese version shall prevail and be conclusive.
10. Important Notes
(1) Applicants may not apply to more than one department, program, or selection scheme at the same time for
each of the Summer and Winter Entrance Examinations.
In addition, applicants who have already passed the Summer Entrance Examinations for the Graduate
School of Information Science and Technology cannot apply for the Winter Entrance Examinations without
first declining the enrollment. Similarly, applicants who have already passed the Winter Entrance
Examinations of the Graduate School of the Information Science and Technology in the previous academic
year, cannot apply for the Summer Entrance Examinations without first declining the enrollment.
(2) Applicants cannot be enrolled in the Graduate School of Information Science and Technology while also
enrolled in other universities (as prescribed in the School Education Law) or other schools at the University
of Tokyo.
(3) Applications will not be accepted if the documents specified in Section 7 above are not submitted in full
by the application deadline. Regarding TOEFL Score submissions, follow the “Guidelines for Submission
of TOEFL Scores (for AY2027 Entrance Examinations), Graduate School of Information Science and
Technology, the University of Tokyo”. After the application process is completed, no changes to the
documents will be accepted and there will be no refund of the examination fee, under any circumstances.
(4) Examination Admission Card is delivered via E-mail to each applicant.
1. For Summer Entrance Examinations, if the Examination Admission Card is not delivered by 17:00 on
Friday, July 17, 2026, contact the Graduate School of Information Science and Technology Admissions
Office (ref. Section 11 below) for instructions on taking the examination. Do not inquire about the arrival
status of the Examination Admission Card before 17:00 on Friday, July 17, 2026.
2. For Winter Entrance Examinations, if the Examination Admission Card is not delivered by 17:00 on
Thursday, December 17, 2026, contact the Graduate School of Information Science and Technology
Admissions Office (ref. Section 11 below) for instructions on taking the examination. Do not inquire about
the arrival status of the Examination Admission Card before 17:00 on Thursday, December 17, 2026.
(5) The University of Tokyo provides reasonable accommodations to ensure that individuals with disabilities,
etc. are not disadvantaged during entrance examinations and studies after enrollment, and provides
consultations for this purpose. Those who wish to request accommodations for entrance examinations
should contact the Graduate School of Information Science and Technology Admissions Office (ref.
Section 11 below) by the application deadline. Although requests made after the above deadline will still
be considered, it is recommended that applicants send their requests well in advance as it may not be
possible to provide certain accommodations without sufficient preparation time. (Please refer to the
admission webpage for more details: https://www.i.u-tokyo.ac.jp/edu/entra/entra_e.shtml)
(6) Applicants with foreign nationalities must obtain a visa permitting enrollment at a Japanese graduate school,
as stipulated in the “Immigration Control and Refugee Recognition Act (Law No. 319, 1951)”, by the time
of enrollment procedures.
(7) Employees of the government, a company, or other corporations wishing to be enrolled at this graduate
11
The document is a translation from the official Japanese version.
In the case of conflict, the Japanese version shall prevail and be conclusive.
school while remaining on the job must submit a letter of approval from the head of the organization at the
time of the enrollment procedures. The letter should show that the head of the organization approves you
to be enrolled as a regular doctoral student at this graduate school. The “head of the organization” can be
anyone who is in a higher position than you. (The exact position does not matter.)
(8) Under some circumstances, application procedures, examination dates, and other details may be subject to
change.
(9) After the application process is complete, no documents or other materials submitted will be returned,
under any circumstances.
(10) After the enrollment procedure is complete, the admission fee will not be refunded under any circumstances.
(11) The University of Tokyo shall use the personal information obtained through the application process only
for the purpose of (1) Selection procedures, (2) Notification of results and (3) Enrollment procedures. For
those who enroll, this personal information and the examination results used for admission selection shall
also be used for (4) Educational Affairs (school registration, attendance, etc.), (5) Student Support
(healthcare, career support, application procedure for tuition exemption and scholarships, use of libraries,
etc.) and (6) the Management of tuition payment.
(12) The examination results used for admission selection may be used in future studies for the improvement of
admission and education systems at the University of Tokyo.
(13) All dates and times stated in this Admission Guide are in Japan Standard Time (JST).
(14) Those who make any false statements in their personal history, application, and/or in any other
accompanying forms, statements or documents may have their admission rescinded even after being
admitted.
(15) In case of an unexpected situation such as natural disasters, please pay attention to the up-to-date
information about entrance examinations posted on the website of this graduate school, etc.
(16) Examination Admission Card and other important notices will be delivered to each applicant via E-mail to
the address stated on the application form. Be sure to configure your E-mail setting so that you can receive
E-mails from the domain u-tokyo.ac.jp and receive notifications when they arrive.
12
The document is a translation from the official Japanese version.
In the case of conflict, the Japanese version shall prevail and be conclusive.
11. Contact Information
If you have any inquiries about entrance examinations, contact us via E-mail instead of calling. We will
reply to your questions during weekdays (except Saturdays, Sundays, and Japanese national holidays) from
9:00 to 17:00 in JST. You should start your application as soon as possible and contact us early if you have
any questions. Note that it can take several days for us to answer your questions. In particular, it may take
a long time to answer your questions during the period just before the Summer Entrance Examinations,
due to the “Obon” holiday season in Japan. During that time, our office may be closed on weekdays, in
addition to Saturdays, Sundays, and national holidays.
Admissions Office,
Graduate School of Information Science and Technology, The University of Tokyo
7-3-1 Hongo, Bunkyo-ku, Tokyo 113-8656 JAPAN
Tel: +81-3-5841-7926
Office hour: 9:00-17:00 except Saturdays, Sundays, and holidays
E-mail: johoriko.t@gs.mail.u-tokyo.ac.jp
URL: https://www.i.u-tokyo.ac.jp/edu/entra/entra_e.shtml
April 2026
The University of Tokyo has established the “The University of Tokyo Security Export Control
Regulations” in accordance with Japan’s “Foreign Exchange and Foreign Trade Act”, and rigorously
implements security export control for potential students before and after their enrollment on the basis of
these regulations. In particular, pre-enrollment screenings are mandatory for all international students and
also for Japanese students in certain circumstances.
Please be aware that applicants who fall under any of the conditions set out in the Act may not receive
permission to enroll at the university or may have their research activities restricted after their enrollment
even if they have passed the entrance examinations.

b) Additional Requirements for Individual Programs
In addition to the General Documents listed in item a) above, individual programs may require the
submission of additional materials, including but not limited to TOEFL and GRE scores. Requirements
vary by program and it is the sole responsibility of the applicant to review and satisfy the application
guidelines and deadlines established by each program. Application guidelines of individual programs can
be found on the following website. https://www.t.u-tokyo.ac.jp/en/study-at-utokyo/soe/international-programs

4. Announcement of Results, and Admission Procedures
(1) Information regarding applicants who are accepted will be posted on the website below. The posting
date on the website will be announced by each program. However, information about International
Graduate Program in the Field of Civil Engineering and Infrastructure Studies (Embassy
Recommendation Course) and CHINA SCHOLARSHIP COUNCIL Doctoral Program (PhD Course)
is not included here.
https://www.t.u-tokyo.ac.jp/en/study-at-utokyo/soe/successful-applicants
(2) Successful applicants must complete the necessary admission procedures by the deadline below.
Please note, if admission procedures are not completed by the deadline, the applicant will be
considered as not entering the school. The deadline for admission procedures is scheduled as
follows but may be subject to change. The finalized dates for admission procedures will be
announced on our website later.
・Deadline
For April Entry: Monday, March 8, 2027 (tentative)
For October Entry: Thursday, September 16, 2027 (tentative)
(3) Admission authorization notification PDFs will be sent to successful applicants via T-cens before the
admission procedures. A hard copy of this paper will be provided to the applicant after they arrive in
Japan.
(4) Admission Fee, Tuition and Exemptions
Successful applicants must pay the following fees during designated admission procedures period
(Note1). It is possible to apply for Admission Fee Exemption and Tuition Exemption at the
University’s admission desk during this period. Submitting an application for admission fee
exemption and/or tuition exemption allows applicants to defer payment of the admission fee and/or
tuition until the screening results are determined (Note 2).
1) Admission Fee 282,000 yen (Note 3)
2) Tuition for the first semester 260,400 yen (Annual Amount 520,800 yen) (Note 3)
Note1 MEXT Scholarship Students (i.e., students who are awarded Japanese Government
Monbukagakusho Scholarships) are exempt from this requirement.
Note2 Applicants should be aware that the chances of getting an admission fee and/or
tuition exemption are low. In some cases, a partial exemption may be granted in lieu
of a full exemption.
Note3 The payment amount is an estimate. After enrollment or during attendance, the
payment amount will be revised, and the new payment amount will be applied from
the time of this revision.
For further details, please refer to
https://www.u-tokyo.ac.jp/en/prospective-students/tuition_fees.html
5. Admission Date
The admission date is scheduled to be on April 1, 2027, or October 1, 2027: however, these dates
are subject to change. Detailed information regarding admission dates will be announced on our
website later.
7
6. Notes
(1) Applicants may apply to only one course across the April 2027 and October 2027 entries in
the International Admission Programs at the Graduate School of Engineering, the University
of Tokyo.
Note: For the list of courses, refer to “1. Introduction: International Admission
Programs and Courses” of this admission guideline.
(2) Applicants cannot be enrolled in other universities or other Schools at the University of Tokyo
at the same time as the Graduate School of Engineering.
(3) Applications will not be accepted if the specified documents are not complete and in order
by the submission date except in extreme, unavoidable circumstances (as determined by the
Graduate School of Engineering). After the application process is complete, no changes to
documents will be accepted and no documents will be returned, under any circumstances.
(4) Those who wish to enter the university while still being employed are required, upon entry,
to submit a Letter of Approval (any format is acceptable) from their employer. The letter must
be issued by someone in a higher position than the applicant, although the specific title does
not matter, and must certify that the applicant’s continued employment status will not hinder
their enrollment to the university.
(5) The University of Tokyo provides reasonable accommodations to ensure that applicants and
students with disabilities or other circumstances requiring accommodations are not
disadvantaged during entrance examinations or in their academic activities after enrollment.
Consultations regarding such accommodations are accepted. Those who wish to request
accommodations for entrance examinations and academic activities must contact the
administrative office of each program by the application deadline. Requests submitted after
the deadline will continue to be considered: however, due to the need for preparation, the
later the request is made, the fewer accommodations can be provided.
(6) Applicants must obtain, prior to entering Japan, a visa that permits enrollment in a Japanese
graduate school, as stipulated in the “Immigration Control and Refugee Recognition Act
(Cabinet Order No. 319 of 1951).”
(7) Under certain circumstances, application procedures, examination dates, and other details
may be subject to change.
(8) Once paid, examination fees and admission fees will not be refunded under any
circumstances.
(9) Personal information will be handled in accordance with the “Act on the Protection of
Personal Information Held by Independent Administrative Agencies” and the “Rules and
Regulations for the Proper Management of Personal Information Held by the University of
Tokyo”.
1. The University of Tokyo shall use the personal information obtained through the
application process only for the purpose of 1) Selection procedures, 2) Notification of
results and 3) Enrollment procedures. For those who enroll, this personal information
and the examination results used for admission selection shall also be used for 1)
Educational Affairs (school registration, attendance, etc.), 2) Student Support
(healthcare, career support, application procedure for tuition exemption and
scholarships, use of libraries, etc.) and 3) the Management of tuition payment.
2. The tasks described in item 1 above will be carried out by the University or in some
cases, by a systems company entrusted with the management of T-cens. Where
necessary, the systems company may be provided with all or part of the personal
information obtained in order to carry out these tasks.
(10) The examination results used in the selection of entrants may be used in future studies for
the improvement of both the entrance examination and the education at the University of
Tokyo.
8
(11) If your application form and submitted documents include any false, misrepresented, or
copied statements and/or information, (including essays and portfolios prepared by other
individuals, or submissions of copied information), your enrollment may be cancelled, even
after entering the school.
(12) Applicants must sign and submit “Graduate School of Engineering, The University of Tokyo
Declaration for Applicants to 2027 International Admission Programs.” The document file is
available on T-cens. If the document is not submitted, the application will not be accepted.
(13) In case of an unexpected situation such as natural disaster, please note that information
related to entrance examinations will be announced on the Graduate School of Engineering
website.
The University of Tokyo has established the “The University of Tokyo Security Export Control
Regulations” in accordance with Japan’s “Foreign Exchange and Foreign Trade Act”, and rigorously
implements security export control for potential students before and after their enrollment on the basis
of these regulations. In particular, pre-enrollment screenings are mandatory for all international
students and also for Japanese students in certain circumstances. Please be aware that applicants
who fall under any of the conditions set out in the Act may not receive permission to enroll at the
university or may have their research activities restricted after their enrollment even if they have
passed the entrance examinations.

3. How to Apply
The periods for acceptance of applications are divided into two categories - Application Schedule A and
Application Schedule B - as indicated below (2).
However, Application Schedule B does not apply to some departments, so please confirm with the
department intended for application in advance. Applicants will not be prevented from applying during both
acceptance periods, but persons wishing to enter the School in October 2026 shall be subject to the
limitations of Application Schedule A.
(1) Applicants must create an entrance application form via the WEB Application System. All required
documents must be converted into PDF format and uploaded to the system. For further details, please
follow the instructions provided on the Graduate School’s website and the Web A pplication System. The
system can be accessed through the Graduate School’s website : https://www.t.u-tokyo.ac.jp/en/study-at-
utokyo/soe/apply/guideline
(2) Application period
Application Schedule A: From May 29 (Friday) until 3:00 p.m., June 4 (Thursday), 2026 (JST)【Strict
Deadline】
Application Schedule B: From November 27 (Friday) until 3:00 p.m., December 3 (Thursday), 2026 (JST)
【Strict Deadline】

This document is a translation from the authoritative Japanese version.
* However, for "Other” in “Documents for Submission,” refer to the “Guide to Entrance Examinations” of
the department to which the application will be sent, as the submission method or period may differ.
4. Examination Schedule and Venue
(1) Application Schedule A:
The Primary examination will be held over five days, from August 31 (Monday) through September 4
(Friday), 2026. For the examination schedule and venue, refer to the “Guide to Entrance Examinations” of
the department to which the application will be sent.
As a general rule, the written examinations of General education subjects (General academic) will be held
at specified venues (on the Hongo campus).
The Secondary examinations will be conducted between mid-January and early February, 2027. Details
regarding examination dates, timetable and venue will be notified to eligible applicants by around early
January 2027. However, for applicants falling under either of the following categories, the secondary
examination will be held over five days, from August 31 (Monday) to September 4 (Friday), 2026.
① Those who have already obtained a master’s degree or professional degree at the time of application,
or who are expected to receive a master’s degree or professional degree by September 30, 2026. (Note
1)
② Those who wish to enroll in October 2026.
Note 1) Secondary examinations will, in some cases, take place sometime between mid-January and early February, even if
candidates fall under ①. Please refer to the “Guide to Entrance Examinations” from the department to which application will
be sent, or to the notice provided along with the examination admission card.
(2) Application Schedule B:
Primary and secondary examinations will be held between mid-January 2027 and early February 2027.
Please refer to the "Guide to Entrance Examinations" of the department to which the application will be
sent. Candidates will be advised of examination dates and other relevant information in December 2026,
when examination admission card notices will be provided.
5. Selection Methods
(1) Selection shall be based on primary and secondary examinations.
(2) Selection for the primary examination shall be based on a written examination, an oral examination, and
submitted documents.
(3) Some departments make selections based on submitted documents. Written examinations, and oral
examinations may be conducted only for those who have passed the selections based on their documents.
Examination fees will not be refunded even if applicants do not pass the document -based selections. For
details, refer to the “Guide to Entrance Examinations” of the department to which the application will be
sent.
(4) The secondary examination shall be in regard to the applicant ’s master's thesis or a related topic. The
secondary examination will only be conducted for those who pass the primary examination.
- 4 -
This document is a translation from the authoritative Japanese version.
(5) Selection of students for admission shall be based on a comprehensive judgment, taking into account the
written examination, the oral examination, submitted documents, the applicant's master's thesis and other
related materials.

subjects on Application Schedule B, please refer to the “Guide to Entrance Examinations” from the

9. Precautionary Notes
(1) Applicants cannot apply to two or more departments or programs within the Graduate School of
Engineering at the same time. Those who have passed the primary examination of the Application Schedule
A of the school cannot apply for Schedule B unless they withdraw from Schedule A. In addition, applicants
cannot be enrolled in other universities or other schools within The University of Tokyo concurrently with
their enrollment in the Graduate School of Engineering.
(2) Applications will not be accepted unless all the required documents are complete and submitted by the
application deadline. After the application process is complete, no changes to documents will be accepted
and no documents will be returned, under any circumstances.
(3) Applicants must print out their examination admission card from the Web Application System: My Page
and bring it on the day of the examination. The examination admission card will be available from:
Application Schedule A: Around 3p.m., Friday, July 3 , 2026
Application Schedule B: Around 3p.m., Tuesday, December 22 , 2026
- 12 -
This document is a translation from the authoritative Japanese version.
(4) Those who wish to enroll while remaining in their current employment status must submit at the time of
admission procedures, a Letter of Approval from their employer (in any format; the specific title of the
person providing approval does not matter as long as they hold a higher position), certifying that there is
no interference with their enrollment while remaining employed.
(5) In the School of Engineering, some departm ents allow admission in October 2026 in addition to April
2027. For more details, refer to the “Guide to Entrance Examinations” of the department to which the
application will be sent.
(6) The University of Tokyo provides reasonable accommodations to ensure that individuals with disabilities ,
etc. are not disadvantaged during entrance examinations and studies after enrollment,and provides
consultations for this purpose. Those who wish to request accommodations for entrance examinations and
studies after enrollment should contact the office of the department where they are applying before
application deadline (refer to 4. How to Apply). Although requests made after the above deadline will still
be considered, it is recommended that applicants send their requests well in advance as it may not be
possible to provide certain accommodations without sufficient preparation time. (Please refer to the
following website for more details: https://www.t.u-tokyo.ac.jp/en/study-at-utokyo/soe/apply/guideline)
(7) Applicants who have foreign nationality must have a visa that will allow admission to a Japanese graduate
school, as stipulated in the “Immigration Control and Refugee Recognition Act (Law No. 319, 1951),” by
the time of admission procedures.
(8) Under some circumstances, application procedures, examination dates and way of conducting, and other
details may be subject to change.
(9) Once paid, examination fees will not be refunded under any circumstances.
(10) After the admission process is completed, admission fees will not be refunded under any circumstances.
(11) The University of Tokyo shall use the personal information obtained through the application process
only for the purpose of ① Selection procedures, ② Notification of results and ③ Enrollment
procedures. For those who enroll, this personal information and the examination results used for
admission selection shall also be used for ④ Educational Affairs (school registration, attendance, etc.),
⑤ Student Support (healthcare, career support, application procedure for tuition exemption and
scholarships, use of libraries, etc.) and ⑥ the Management of tuition payment.
(12) The examination results used for admission selection may be used in future studies for the improvement
of admission and education systems at the University of Tokyo.
(13) In case of an unexpected situation such as a natural disaster, please note that information related to
entrance examinations will be offered on the Graduate School of Engineering website.
10. Response to fraud
If any false statement or forgery is found in the application documents, or if clear evidence of any
fraudulent activity during the examination is found, admission and enrollment may be canceled even after
the applicant has been accepted or has already entered this Graduate School.

Inquiries regarding the ‘Guidelines for Applicants’ should be submitted via the ‘Graduate

The University of Tokyo has established the “The University of Tokyo Security Export Control
Regulations” in accordance with Japan’s “Foreign Exchange and Foreign Trade Act”, and
rigorously implements security export control for potential students before and after their
enrollment on the basis of these regulations. In particular, pre -enrollment screenings are
mandatory for all international students and also for Japanese students in certain circumstances.
Please be aware that applicants who fall under any of the conditions set out in the Act may not
receive permission to enroll at the university or may have their research activities restricted after
their enrollment even if they have passed the entrance examinations.

Prospectus
Prospectus for 2027
This special program offers non-Japanese students an opportunity to work toward Doctor of Engineering (3 years) or Master of Engineering (2 years) degrees. Top qualified students who passed the September Application will be awarded scholarships including the Japanese Government's Scholarship (MEXT: The Ministry of Education, Culture, Sports, Science and Technology) and other equivalent scholarships provided by IME Graduate Program. We also seek for highly qualified students to enroll as self-supporting students. All of the students will be enrolled in one of the departments below.
Mechanical Engineering
Electrical Engineering and Information Systems
Materials Engineering
Aeronautics and Astronautics
Chemical System Engineering
Precision Engineering
Bioengineering (Doctor course only)
For master course application, please visit the following site:
http://www.global30.t.u-tokyo.ac.jp/
Program Features
Lectures and research guidance in English
Interdisciplinary program
Direct entry to postgraduate degree courses
(Non-degree or research fellowship programs are not offered.)
Admission based on the evaluation of the documents submitted by the applicants and examination and interviews conducted by the faculty via e-mail and Zoom
(You don't have to come over to Japan to take Regular Entrance Examination)
Free basic Japanese language classes for the students and their spouses
Tuition and Fees
Entrance Fee
282,000 yen (as of 2026)
Annual Tuition Fee
535,800 yen (Master) (as of 2026)
520,800 yen (Doctor) (as of 2026)
Examination Fee (at the time of online application)
10,000 yen
Students awarded the MEXT scholarships are exempt from the above fees, therefore, the examination fee will be refunded upon enrollment.
Scholarship
Top qualified September Applicants who have exceptional academic records will be awarded the Japanese Government's MEXT scholarship by recommendation of the University of Tokyo or scholarships provided by IME Graduate Program for the period of either the Doctoral program or the Masters program. The MEXT Scholarship grantees are exempt from Entrance Fee and Annual Tuition Fees throughout their entire term of study.
MEXT Scholarship Amount(Basic Monthly Stipend + Additional Stipend) (as of 2026)
Basic Monthly Stipend
status

2,000 yen
Kashiwa Campus
*The additional stipend varies according to the student's "principal place of research," regardless of place of residence.
The MEXT Scholarship will not be offered to students who are already receiving grants from other sources.
If you apply for the Japanese Government's MEXT Scholarship by obtaining the recommendation of the Japanese Embassy in your country, you will not be eligible to enroll in this program. Instead, you will have to initially enroll as a research student and then later take a regular entrance examination to be admitted to the graduate courses.

Financial Supports for Self-Supporting Students
IME Students who are not awarded any scholarship, including the scholarships other than the ones offered by IME Graduate program, are called self-supporting students: they are responsible for their own fees and living expenses.
After entering The University of Tokyo, self-supporting students have chances to apply for several kinds of private scholarships. However, please note that many well qualified students compete for these scholarships and that the scholarship amounts are often lower than the MEXT Scholarship. Basically there are almost no scholarships available before your enrollment.
http://ois.t.u-tokyo.ac.jp/students_life/selfsupported.html
Self-supporting students can apply for the exemption of Entrance Fee and Annual Tuition, Entrance Fee could be exempted up to a half of the full amount. Annual Tuition could be exempted a half or the whole of the total amount.
https://www.u-tokyo.ac.jp/en/prospective-students/exemptions.html
SEUT RA Fellowship : Type S, Type A and Type B (only for doctoral course student)
http://www.t.u-tokyo.ac.jp/soee/admission/seut_ra.html
Dormitory Information
http://ois.t.u-tokyo.ac.jp/dormitory/index.html
Admission
The Graduate Council of the University of Tokyo grants admission to this program based upon the recommendation of the Graduate School of Engineering. Screening for admission is based both on the evaluation of documents submitted by the international students and, if necessary, on the internet interviews and examinations conducted by the faculty. There are three application periods (July application for April Enrollment, September application and January application for October Enrollment). When the evaluation process is complete, applicants who are evaluated as qualified and meet the IME criteria will be granted admission to this program. At September Application, top qualified applicants among them will be nominated as candidates for scholarships provided by IME Graduate Program.
Schedule
The program has three application rounds per year; however, applicants may apply only once per year.
All times and dates indicated below are based on Japan Standard Time (JST).

Additional conditions and regulations regarding the MEXT scholarship
Required Application Documents

AF1([Form 1] – [Form 7] and [Form Recommend] ):
[Form 2]: Please provide your academic grades (1-6) as stated officially in your transcript. If your school doesn’t provide you with GPA and if you can calculate it yourself, please calculate it and fill it in. In that case, please be sure to attach the conversion chart when you submit the transcript. (Please refer to no.10, 11 as well.).
[Form 3]: TOEFL is preferable. If you already have IELTS score or if it is difficult for you to take TOEFL test, we will accept IELTS score. GRE is required by IME Graduate Program. GMAT is not acceptable. If the score of TOEFL/IELTS or GRE is not ready at the time of filling T-cens application form, please fill in the date when you took or will take the examination. (Please refer to no.17 and no.18 as well.)
[Form 5]: It is essential to find and write one or two prospective supervisor(s) whose research field matches yours. (Please refer to no.20 as well.)
[Form Recommend]: IME Graduate Program requires two recommendation letters from the faculty members of the university you belong to or graduated from. (Please refer to no.8, 9)
[Note] Please be sure to confirm the information you have written and sign on the last page of the forms.

Both must be the faculty member of the university and one of them must be your direct academic supervisor. It would be preferable if the other is from Dean or head of Department of the university you belong to or graduated from.
10,11
Full Academic Transcript (Bachelor’s & Master’s)
If you haven’t completed your course yet, you are requested to submit all the academic transcript you have received by the time of application.
One original or one certified copy (with certified seal/stamp of the university you belong to or graduated from) is required. Photocopy without certified seal/stamp will not be accepted.
If GPA is not stated in the transcript, and if your school can issue the authorized certificate of your GPA or your class rank, please submit it together with the transcript. If your school doesn’t provide you with GPA and if you calculate it yourself, please submit the document that shows how you calculated it.
12,13,14
Certificate of Graduation / Diploma, Certificate of Degree, or Certificate of Expected Graduation
One certified copy (with certified seal/stamp of the university you belong to or graduated from) is required. Photocopy without certified seal/stamp will not be accepted.
If the Degree is indicated in the Certificate of Graduation / Diploma, then you do not need to upload the Certificate of Degree.
Those who have not yet been conferred with the diploma in the present course of study at the time of application are requested to submit a certificate of expected graduation.
15
Personal Identification/Passport
A photocopy of your valid passport (the most preferable), census register, or other proof of citizenship. If the document is written in language other than English or Japanese, please attach English translation. You can translate by yourself.
16
Letter of Declaration
(When you submit the application document, please download this letter from T-cens.)
17
English Language Proficiency:
Please submit (A), (B), or (C).
TOEFL (or IELTS): One copy of your TOEFL score is required. You do not have to arrange to send the official score report from ETS to the University of Tokyo.
If you already have received your original　TOEFL score report, please upload it in your T-cens page and send the copy of it by post.
If you have not received your official TOEFL score report at the time of online application, please upload the online score report in your T-cens page and send the copy of the printed online score report by post. As soon as you receive the official TOEFL score report, please send the scanned copy to the IME Graduate Program Office by email.
The validity period for scores of the document is within 2 years at the deadline of the documents submission.
For the applicant of Precision Engineering Department, please refer to the following site regarding the TOEFL/IELTS criteria.
http://www.pe.t.u-tokyo.ac.jp/en/admission.html#IME
The Certificate of English as the Medium of Instruction from your university. One original or one certified copy (with certified seal/stamp of the university you belong to or graduated from) is required. Photocopy without certified seal/stamp will not be accepted. If it is stated in your transcript, you do not have to submit an extra document. In that case, please underline the part which explains it.
If English is your native language, you do not have to submit any of the above document. Instead, please submit proof of citizenship, such as a copy of your valid passport.
18
GRE Score (GRE General test)
Please arrange so that ETS Center can directly send it to the University of Tokyo.
DI (Designated Institution) code of the University of Tokyo Engineering: 8596
Please upload the online score report in your T-cens page and send the printed online score report by post.
Even if you arrange to send the official score report to us from ETS, please send the printed online score report by post. In case that the online score is not ready when you ship out the paper-based application documents, please send the scanned copy of it to the IME Graduate Program Office after you receive it by email as long as you can send it to us by the deadline of submission of paper-based application documents.
The validity period for scores of the document is within 5 years at the deadline of the documents submission.
For the applicant of Precision Engineering Department, please refer to the following site regarding the GRE criteria.
http://www.pe.t.u-tokyo.ac.jp/en/admission.html#IME
If it is impossible for you to submit GRE score before the deadline, please refer HERE.

A copy of Examination Fee payment receipt
(10,000 Japanese Yen is required for the examination fee.)
‘How to make Payment of Examination Fee at E-Shiharai.net’

Acceptance Letter from your prospective supervisor. (Exept Mechanical Engineering)
21

Enclose this Submission Checklist whenever you send materials to the ICT Admissions Desk via post.
Checklist (.pdf)
23
Laboratory Assignment Preference Form (Only the Master' Program of Mechanical Engineering)
Laboratory Assignment Preference Form
Application Procedures
IME Graduate Program applicants must apply through the T-cens, an online application system for the Graduate School of Engineering, the University of Tokyo. First, please read all the following procedures carefully, and follow the instructions step by step according to the numbers. Please never skip any steps.
Before Starting Any Application:
If you experience some difficulties while working with T-cens or do not have stable online access, please check the following link:
T-cens recommended computer settings.
Deadline for each Application Procedure (month/date/year)
July Application
September Application
January Application
Application Procedure 1-9
8/19/2026
10/14/2026
2/17/2027
Application Procedure 10-11
9/21/2026
10/28/2026
3/3/2027
Application Procedure 12
9/9/2026
11/4/2026
3/10/2027
IME Application Procedure 1: Find your prospective supervisor at the University of Tokyo.
You must find your prospective supervisor whose research field matches yours from the academic staff involved in IME Graduate Program from Academic Staff List ( http://www.ime.t.u-tokyo.ac.jp/index.php?id=5 ) .
[Note 1]
Only those who receive an acceptance letter from prospective supervisor are eligible to proceed to the next application step for IME Graduate Program. It is essential to receive the acceptance letter prior to submitting paper based application forms by post. The application forms submitted without having received the acceptance letter cannot be accepted. To make your application smooth, we strongly recommend you contact your prospective supervisor before your application. It will take plenty of time to contact the prospective supervisor and receive the acceptance letter, so please start your on-line application well in advance.
Choose up to two suitable professors. You are supposed to write the names in the application Form 5,“Name of faculty member”. Please fill in at least one professor.
[Note 2]
Applicants of Department of Mechanical Engineering don’t need to receive the acceptance letter, instead there is the first screening.
Applicants to Master’s Program in Department of Mechanical Engineering are required to submit Laboratory Assignment Preference From.
Laboratory Assignment Preference Form
IME Application Procedure 2: Create your T-cens account.
You can create your T-cens account by clicking the blue T-cens icon below.
IME Application Procedure 3: Visit your account page:
After creating your T-cens account, you will soon receive the e-mail at your registered e-mail address with the URL of Login page as well as Login ID and Password. If you cannot find the e-mail from T-cens in-box or trash box, please contact ICT Support Desk. And whenever you have technical problems, you can also contact ICT Support Desk:
< email: ICTsupportdesk@t-adm.t.u-tokyo.ac.jp >
[Note]
Please keep this e-mail from T-cens. You will need the information there for various purposes later on until your entrance formalities become complete.
T-cens system messages will be sent to your account page when necessary. Each time a new message is sent to your account page, an e-mail is sent to your registered e-mail address. Please be sure to read a new message whenever you receive one.
Again, whenever you contact IME Graduate Program Office or ICT Support Desk, please only use the e-mail you used to make your T-cens account. When you need to change your e-mail address, firstly please inform the Offices about the change of e-mail address.
Your T-cens Application Number (Registration No. + Course Code) can be found under the space for your photo on your acount page. Please clarify your T-cens Application Number in your e-mail subject line whenever you contact the IME Graduate Program Office or ICT Support Desk.
IME Application Procedure 4: Fill in the Application Forms in T-cens.
Click “Apply” icon on your account page, and fill in Online Application Forms from Form 1 to Form 7. After you complete all the Forms and save them, please proceed to “File Upload”.
[Note]
Before you switch to a different Form, please press the “SAVE” button. Otherwise all the information you have filled in will be erased.
IME Application Procedure 5: Upload files.
Upload your photo.
File should be:
Under 500kB
jpg
Photo should be:
the one taken within 6 months prior to the application.
in a plain background.
without sunglasses or hats.
Download and fill in AF0, and AF2 - AF6, and upload them.
Upload all the other required files on the list.
[Note]
All the files should be PDF file and less than 2MB each except for master thesis which should be less than 10MB. (Please upload the image files scanned by a scanner. Photo files taken by a camera are not acceptable.)
The name of the files you are going to upload should be completely the same as the one on the list. Otherwise, you will not be able to upload the file.
You can replace the uploaded file with a new file until you press the COMPLETE button on “Submit” page. ( Do not press the COMPLETE button till IME Application Procedure 7 is finished.)
If you need to replace the files after pressing the COMPLETE button, please contact IME Graduate Program Office.
IME Application Procedure 6: Complete Recommendation Documents.
Please fill in the required information of your referees.
[Note]
Your referees must be the members of the faculty of the university you belong to or graduated from. Both must be the faculty member of the university and one of them must be your direct academic supervisor.
You MUST get an approval from both of your referees before filling in their information.
Soon after you register your referees, they are supposed to receive an e-mail from T-cens with the information to start uploading recommendation documents. Please confirm whether your referees have received the e-mail from T-cens system, and if not, please contact ICT Support Desk and ask for their assistance.
< email: ICTsupportdesk@t-adm.t.u-tokyo.ac.jp >
Both referees have to finish uploading recommendation documents before you move on to IME Application Procedure 8.
Both referees have to finish uploading recommendation documents by the IME Online Application Deadline. Please check the status of your own recommendation letters on your responsibility and make sure that they can submit the recommendation documents online by the deadline.
IME Application Procedure 7: Pay Examination Fee by Credit Card.
You must make payment of 10,000 yen by credit card by the IME Online Application Deadline.
[Note]
Please refer to ‘How to make Payment of Examination Fee at E-Shiharai.net’.
It is possible to use a credit card which carries a name different from that of the applicant. Those who are not able to make a payment using credit cards must contact the IME Graduate Program Office no later than 2 weeks prior to the IME Online Application Deadline.
After the examination, MEXT scholarship candidates who are selected via recommendation by the University will receive a refund of the examination fee directly to their account upon enrollment.
IME Application Procedure 8: Press "Submit" tab and "Submit" button.
Click “Submit” tab at the top, and review all the contents of your online application documents as well as the status of the recommendation letters. After confirming the contents you have written in the application form and confirming that both of your referees have already submitted the recommendation documents online, please check in the squares in the checklist, and press the “Submit” button at the bottom.
IME Application Procedure 9: Contact IME Graduate Program Office.
(Be sure to send the e-mail from the same e-mail address you used to make your T-cens account.)
Please inform IME Graduate Program Office of your T-cens Applicant ID Number and the completion of IME Application Procedure 1-8. Then IME Graduate Program Office will arrange so that your prospective supervisor could view your online application documents.
IME Application Procedure 10: Contact the prospective supervising professor.
(Be sure to send the e-mail from the same e-mail address you used to make your T-cens account.)
After you receive a reply from IME Graduate Program Office, you can proceed to contact your prospective supervisor and ask whether he/she could accept you in his/her laboratory. This procedure is considered to be a preliminary evaluation of your qualification. Please understand that not all the applicants are able to receive the acceptance letter. When your prospective supervisor agrees to accept you, please contact IME Graduate Program Office by forwarding his/her e-mail. IME Office will ask him/her to send the acceptance letter directly to you and IME Office by e-mail.
[Note]
Please do not contact your prospective supervisor until you receive the reply from IME Graduate Program Office.
Please inform your prospective supervisor of your T-cens Applicant ID Number and introduce yourself.
You must contact the prospective supervisors one by one.
If you do not receive any reply from the prospective supervisor, please contact the IME Graduate Office.
IME Application Procedure 11: Receive an acceptance letter.
When your prospective supervisor agrees to accept you or when you receive the electronic version of the acceptance letter, please forward it to IME Graduate Program Office immediately.
[Note1]
Being able to receive the acceptance letter means that you pass the first screening.
When professor has declined, please contact the IME Graduate Office as well.
Only those who received the acceptance letter could proceed to the Application Procedure 12.
[Note2]
The applicants of Mechanical Engineering DO NO NEED to receive the acceptance letter. Department of Mechanical Engineering conducts the first screening and only who pass the first screening proceed to the final selection. The schedule of the first screening is as follow:
July Application: August 25th, 2026
September Application: October 22nd, 2026
January Application: February 24th, 2027
IME Application Procedure 12: Submit paper based application documents.
Please send all the required documents to IME Graduate Program Office by EMS or another international delivery service so that they can arrive at the Office on time.
The address of IME Graduate Program Office:
http://www.ime.t.u-tokyo.ac.jp/contactus.html
[Note]
Refer to the checklist and prepare the documents as requested.
Please DO NOT STAPLE your documents.
Please DO NOT PRINT ON BOTH SIDES.
Please send documents in A4 paper size only except in a case to send original document (such as certificate) which is not A4 paper size.
As soon as you ship out the package of yo

This document is a translation of the authoritative Japanese version.
Entrance Examination Guide for the 2027 Academic Year,
Department of Mechanical Engineering,
Graduate School of Engineering,
The University of Tokyo
Master’s Program (Application Schedule A)
Doctoral Program (Application Schedules A and B)
･In case that the contents of this entrance examination guide should change, the revised
guide will be published on the websites of the School of Engineering;
(https://www.t.u-tokyo.ac.jp/en/study-at-utokyo/soe/apply)
and the Department of Mechanical Engineering;
(https://www2.mech.t.u-tokyo.ac.jp/admissions/?lang=en). Please confirm these websites as
needed.
Inquiries about this guide:
7-3-1 Hongo, Bunkyo-ku, Tokyo 113-8656
Department of Mechanical Engineering Administrative Office, Graduate School of
Engineering, The University of Tokyo
Tel: 03-5841-6301
Email: kyoumu@office.mech.t.u-tokyo.ac.jp
This guide serves as a supplement to the “Guidelines for Applicants to the 2027 Master's
Program of the Graduate School of Engineering, The University of Tokyo” and the “Guidelines
for Applicants to the 2027 Doctoral Program of the Graduate School of Engineering, The
University of Tokyo.” Applicants must read this guide along with the aforementioned
application guidelines.
1. Admission Schedule
(Application Schedule A)
・For the master’s program, the qualified applicants who are expected to graduate from a
university by March 31, 2027, will be enrolled in April 2027, and the qualified applicants
who are already graduated from a university at the time they apply or expected to
graduate by September 30, 2026, may be enrolled in either October 2026 or April 2027.
・For the doctoral program, the qualified applicants who are expected to complete a
graduate school master’s program by March 31, 2027, will be enrolled in April 2027, and
the qualified applicants who already completed a graduate school master’s program or are
expected to complete it by September 30, 2026, may be enrolled in either October 2026 or
April 2027.
(Application Schedule B)
･Applicants for the Application Schedule B examination will only be enrolled in April 2027.
2. Supervisors
Faculty members indicated by a diamond (◇) in the faculty list cannot serve as supervisors
for students in any program. Faculty members indicated by a double circle (◎) cannot serve
as supervisors for students in the doctoral program.
Note: The faculty list is accurate as of April 2026 and is subject to change.
(1) Master’s program
The applicants to the master’s program who choose the special oral selection in “3.
Master’s Program” must fill out the inquiry sheet and submit it when submitting the
application form. The applicants who do not choose the special oral selection must fill out
the inquiry sheet, which will be sent with the examination admission card, and submit it
by the indicated deadline. The choice of supervisor will be based on the results of the
examination and the requests from all the applicants, thus the choice of supervisor will
not necessarily be the same as the first choice of an applicant. Please read the instructions
on the inquiry sheet carefully and fill in the names of faculty members whom you wish to
be your supervisor.
(2) Doctoral program
The applicants to the doctoral program must first receive consent from a prospective
supervisor and discuss issues including their research topics before submitting their
applications. The applicants are requested to submit the specified confirmation note
issued by their prospective supervisors with their applications. It is also possible to consult
with the Department Chair regarding the selection of supervisors.
Department Chair: Professor Gentiane VENTURE Tel: +81-3-5841-6355
3. Master’s Program (Application Schedule A only)
In the entrance examination for the 2027 academic year (held in 2026), the "Special Oral
Selection" is introduced for applicants who wish to enroll in the Master's Program in the
Department of Mechanical Engineering at the Graduate School of Engineering, The
University of Tokyo.
Applicants who choose the special oral selection when submitting the application form will
be screened (document screening) and will take a special oral examination in July 2026. For
those who are selected by the special oral selection, supervisors will be assigned and the
written examination will be exempted (the interview test will not be exempted). Those who
are not selected by the special oral selection will automatically become eligible for the
written examination (regular admission) to be held from late August to early September
2026.
Those who do not choose the special oral selection at the time of application will be eligible
for the regular admission.
(1) Special oral selection
For information on the special oral selection and forms for submission, refer to the
Department of Mechanical Engineering website
(https://www.t.u-tokyo.ac.jp/en/soe/admission/general-guideline).
(a) Document screening
In late June, applicants who choose the special oral selection are screened by the
following documents i) through vi), which are submitted to the Department of
Mechanical Engineering at the time of application from late May to early June 2026.
The result will be notified promptly after the completion of the document screening.
i) Special oral selection Form A for the Department of Mechanical Engineering
ii) Transcripts
Prospective graduates must submit transcripts showing grades from the first to
third year of university. However, prospective graduates of the two mechanical
engineering departments of The University of Tokyo (Department of Mechanical
Engineering and Department of Mechano-Informatics) are not required to submit
these documents (those who have already graduated from the two mechanical
engineering departments of The University of Tokyo are required to submit these
documents).
iii) Documents showing the number of credits for graduation up to the 4th year of
university (may be omitted if the transcript shows this information) (not required
for graduates).
iv) Special oral selection Form B (inquiry sheet) for the Department of Mechanical
Engineering.
v) Special oral selection Form C for the Department of Mechanical Engineering.
vi) Official English score (TOEFL-iBT or TOEFL-iBT Home Edition *).
(b) Special oral examination
Only applicants who pass document screening will be allowed to take the special oral
examination online by late July.
The results of the special oral selection will be notified promptly after the special oral
examination. Selected applicants will also be notified of the supervisor to be assigned.
Applicants who are not selected by the special oral selection will automatically be
eligible for the written examination from late August to early September 2026.
(2) Regular admission
(a) Written examination
The written examination for the Master’s Program covers a) general education subject
(mathematics), b) foreign language (please submit your TOEFL score*), and c) the
following specialized subjects (mechanical engineering).
Specialized Subject Fields
Mechanical Questions are primarily from the fields of thermal engineering and
engineering (part 1) fluid engineering.
Questions are primarily from the fields of material mechanics,
Mechanical
mechanical dynamics/control engineering and mechanical
engineering (part 2)
design/production engineering.
International students and examinees will be provided with an English version of the
exam questions for the specialized subject (mechanical engineering) upon request. An
English version of the exam questions for the general education subject (mathematics)
will be provided. For the examination in mathematics, select and answer three out of
six questions.
(b) Interview test
The interview test will only be held for candidates selected through the special oral
examination and for those who have passed the written examination, the results of
which will be announced at 12:30 p.m. on September 4 (Friday). The candidates will be
asked questions about their desired specialized field, research topics, supervisor choice,
as well as other subjects. The candidate will be called in the order that is announced at
the designated meeting place on the examination day. Please note that the candidates
who are not present when called will be disqualified from the interview test.
Note that even those who have been selected by the special oral selection and are
exempted from the written examination will ultimately fail the interview test if they
are absent.
(3) Application schedule
Please see the “Guidelines for Applicants to the 2027 Master's Program Graduate School
of Engineering, The University of Tokyo.”
4. Doctoral Program
(1) Primary examination
(a) Written examination
The written examination in the primary examination for the doctoral program covers a)
foreign language (English TOEFL score* must be submitted) and b) the specialized
contents for the doctoral program (the contents related to each examinee’s specialized
field). However, candidates who have completed or are expected to complete a master’s
degree at the Graduate School of Engineering, The University of Tokyo, are exempted
from a) the foreign language examination and only need to take the b) the specialized
contents for the doctoral program (the contents related to each examinee’s the
specialized field).
Both Japanese and English versions of the exam questions for the doctoral program
specialized content examination (content related to your specialized field) will be
provided.
(b) Oral examination
The examination includes questions about a) one of the academic fields in the
specialized subject (mechanical engineering) that is stated in the specified confirmation
note issued by your prospective supervisor, b) the progress on your master’s thesis or
equivalent research and the research plans of the subject you wish to address, as well
as your knowledge of mechanical engineering related to your research. Howev

Notes:
※ Applicants who passed the Special Oral Examination for Master's Course will be exempted from taking
the English and written specialized subject examinations (However, they will not be exempted from the

provided in the enclosure with the examination admission card.
＊ There will be no master's program examination on Friday, September 4, 2026.
2 Examination Subjects (1) Preliminary Selection
Depending on the number of applicants, preliminary selection may be conducted based on the
“Questionnaire, Department of Electrical Engineering and Information Systems” and “Transcripts
EEIS：Department of Electrical Engineering and Information Systems 3
and Grade Summary Sheets” of the applicants. Applicants who pass the preliminary selection
may take the specialized subject and oral examinations. We will not answer any inquiries
regarding the criteria for the selection.
The results of the preliminary selection will be posted on the department website
(http://www.eeis.t.u-tokyo.ac.jp/en/index.html) by Friday, August 14, 2026.
(2) Special Oral Examination for Master's Course (applied person only)
A special oral examination for master's program is given to applicants whose grades are
evaluated as excellent and whose first choice is to enroll in this department. Those who are
selected in this examination will be exempted from the English examination and the written
examination in specialized subjects of the general entrance examination. However, they will
not be exempted from the standard oral examination.
Please refer to the details of the online examination on the "Applicants Only" page enclosed
with the examination admission card, and prepare the admission card and the equipments
necessary for the examination. In addition to your research field of interest and the reasons
for it, you will be asked about the basic knowledge of that field. You may be asked about your
graduation research and your answer to the questionnaire (see 5) as well.
(3) Specialized Subject (Electrical & Electronic Engineering and Information Engineering)
The test will cover the basic knowledge needed to conduct graduate-level research in electrical
& electronic engineering and information engineering. The problems are written in English or
Japanese. The answers can be given in English or Japanese.
Examinees are requested to select and answer two problems from the following. The time of
examination is 150 minutes for the two problems in total.
Electromagnetism, Electrical Circuits, Information (I), Information (II), Solid-State Physics,
Control and Electric Energy Engineering.
For more details and past examination problems, please refer to the department website
(http://www.eeis.t.u-tokyo.ac.jp/en/index.html). Sample problems are also available on the
department website. (They are not for sale in the print center of the School of Engineering).
(4) Oral Examination
The oral examination is conducted online as a part of the application process. Please
prepare the admission card and the necessary equipments for the online examination by
referring to the details of the online test, which will be available on "Applicants Only" page
provided in the enclosure with the examination admission card. The topics covered in the
oral examination include the examinee's motivation for the application, research theme in
the undergraduate school, basic knowledge of the field to which the examinee is applying,
Questionnaire, Department of Electrical Engineering and Information Systems, and the results
of the written examinations.
An oral examination will also be conducted for those selected in (2) above. We will ask
questions about the examination in (2) and the progress of their graduation research.
EEIS：Department of Electrical Engineering and Information Systems 4
Entrance Examination Guide for the Master's Program
3 Supervisor Preference Survey The supervisor preference survey will be conducted online at the time of application for the
entrance examination. Please refer to the department website (https://www.eeis.t.u-tokyo.ac.jp/
en/staff/) for a list of supervisors. Applicants to the master's program are required to fill out the
“Supervisor Preference Survey for Dept. of EEIS Entrance Exam. [Master Course]”. In accordance
with the University of Tokyo’s Export Control Regulations, if the content of an application is
subject to regulatory control, assignment to the applicant’s preferred academic supervisor may
be restricted. Assignment is determined comprehensively based on the results of the written
and oral examinations, the contents of the submitted documents, and the applicant’s stated
supervisor preferences. If you are a research student already belonging to one in the supervisor
list, please enter your current host professor as the first choice.
Applicants who pass the examination will be notified of their supervisor assignment in a timely
manner. Applicants who have passed the examination and decide not to enroll in the graduate
school should notify the Graduate School Team at the Office of Academic Affairs by submitting
a document (Letter Declining Offer of Admission) as soon as possible.
4 Important Notice Successful applicants may choose to start their program in October, provided that the
applicants fulfill the entrance requirements designated by the School of Engineering. For
oversea applicants, it usually takes about three months to obtain a new status of residence
after getting notification of admission. There is a possibility that you will not have enough
time to enter in October. If you need to apply for a new status of residence upon entering
this Graduate School, please consider selecting April entry. The Department of Electrical
Engineering and Information Systems is not able to arrange visas for overseas applicants
who wish to take the entrance examination. Entrance examination results may be utilized to
improve future entrance examinations and education at the University of Tokyo.

of Electrical Engineering and the preliminary selection, please explain a research content that you desire to conduct in the
Information Systems Master's program. Additionally, please describe the theme of your graduation research and
plans after graduation.

－ 1 －
 Education and research objectives of the Department of Chemical System Engineering
Our department aims to produce globally competitive engineers and researchers in the discipline of
chemical system engineering, which focuses on the design, control, and analysis of chemical
phenomena across scales ranging from the molecular to the global. Our department aims to contribute
to a sustainable society through problem-solving research in areas such as the environment, energy,
safety, and medicine. These goals represent the core educational and research objectives of our
department.
 Department website:
https://www.chemsys.t.u-tokyo.ac.jp/?lang=en
 Information about the entrance examinations including past exam problems:
https://www.chemsys.t.u-tokyo.ac.jp/curriculum/graduate/admission/?lang=en
 Briefing sessions for the entrance examination:
The details of the briefing sessions for the entrance exam will be announced on the above department
website.
To enroll in the Graduate School of Engineering, applicants should complete the Entrance Application
Form using the Admission Application Form Creator available on the Graduate School of
Engineering Application Guidelines website.
Note: Please check the latest version of the guide posted on the department website.

1) The application form and other required documents should be prepared using the Web Application
System and uploaded online.
2) See page 7 for submission of official TOEFL scores.
3) Prior to the examination dates, document screening will be conducted based on the documents enclosed
with the application form. Applicants will be notified of acceptance or rejection by e-mail when the
examination admission card is issued in early July.
4) Refer to the “Examination Schedule” (page 10).
5) The examinee ID number of successful applicants will be posted on the website of the Graduate School
of Engineering.
6) Details will be announced in due course. For applicants who have already obtained a master's degree or
a professional degree at the time of application, or who are expected to receive a master’s degree by
September 30, 2026, the secondary examination will be conducted concurrently with the primary
examination.
－ 3 －
2. Examination Location
The examination will be held at the Hongo Campus, The University of Tokyo. Please refer to the “Campus
Map for Examination” (page 11).
(1) The examination rooms for the subjects to be taken by each applicant will be announced when the
examination admission card is issued.
(2) Examinees are required to enter the designated examination room or waiting room at least 30 minutes
before the start of the written and oral examinations. Examinees arriving late must report immediately
to the proctor.
(3) Examinees who are less than 30 minutes late for the start of each examination will be allowed to take
the examination. Examinees who are late for the start of the oral examination will not be allowed to take
the examination.
3. Items to Bring
(1) Items that must be brought: Examination admission card; black pencil (or mechanical pencil); eraser.
(2) Items that may be brought: A calculator capable of performing exponential and logarithmic calculations
(mobile phones including smart phones may not be used as a calculator); pencil sharpeners (not desk-
top type); watches (with timekeeping functions only).
(3) Mobile phones must be turned off before entering the examination room and placed in a bag or purse.
Mobile phones may not be used as watches or calculators.
(4) Other instructions may be provided separately to applicants for the doctoral program.
4. Notes on the examination
(1) Once the examination has begun, examinees will not be allowed to leave the room even if they have
completed all the answers or wish to forfeit the examination.
(2) The examination admission card must be placed on the desk at all times during the examination.
(3) Answer sheets and problem booklets must not be taken out of the examination room.
5. Other Notes
(1) Any changes to an applicant’s e-mail address or current address after submission of the application
must be reported immediately to Managing Director.
(2) Successful applicants will be announced on the website of the Graduate School of Engineering using
their examinee ID numbers. Inquiries about examination status by telephone or e-mail will not be
accepted.
(3) Laboratory assignments for successful applicants will be announced on the website and bulletin board
of the Department of Chemical System Engineering at Engineering Building No. 5. Applicants will
also be contacted by e-mail. Inquiries by telephone will not be accepted.
(4) Successful applicants must immediately contact the supervisor of the laboratory to which they have
been assigned.
－ 4 －
Master’s Program, Admission Application Guide
1. Applicants must be eligible for admission to the master’s program, regardless of their academic major
or year of qualification.
2. Applicants must select the laboratory of their choice from the list of the department laboratories.
3. Applicants must download and fill in the Reasons for Application and Career Plan from the department
website and submit together with the application form. Applicants must contact the laboratory of their
first choice in advance and receive detailed guidance and permission to apply from the prospective
supervisor. Laboratory preferences have no bearing on admission decisions. Successful applicants will
be notified of their laboratory assignment after the announcement of admission results.
4. Document screening will be conducted based on the documents submitted with the application form to
comprehensively assess the applicant’s basic academic skills and suitability for the Department of
Chemical System Engineering. Applicants will be notified of the results of the document screening by
e-mail when the examination admission card is issued in early July.
5. Foreign applicants who have graduated or are expected to graduate from a foreign university may apply
for the Special Selection for Foreign Students. They should contact the desired supervisor in advance to
obtain permission to apply under this category. The examination subjects for the Special Selection are
the same as those for general admission. Successful applicants for the Special Selection will be assigned
to the laboratory of their desired supervisor.
6. Only those who have passed the document screening can take the following examination subjects A–C.
Note that failure to take all of the required subjects will result in disqualification.
7. Admission decisions will be made based on a comprehensive evaluation of the results of examination
subjects A–C.
A. Foreign Languages (English): Maximum Score 120 points
Applicants must take the TOEFL iBT/TOEFL iBT Home Edition or the TOEIC Listening & Reading
test. Score from the TOEFL or TOEIC L&R tests may not be substituted with scores from any other
foreign language (English) examinations. Details regarding the submission of official scores are
provided on page 7, “Notes regarding Submission of Official Language (English) Examination Scores”
and in the document titled “AY 2027 Graduate School of Engineering Entrance Examinations
Guidelines for Submission of TOEFL Scores”, which can be downloaded from the Graduate School of
Engineering website.
B．Written Examination: Maximum Score 450 points
